3. A.  CLOSED SESSION 
  CONSIDERATION:  Negotiations  
The Board must pass a resolution to go into closed session .  Following the 
passage of the resolution, the Board of Education will go into closed session to 
discuss negotiations.  The regular meeting will reconvene following closed 
session to address additional business.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
Be it therefore resolved that, as permitted in the Michigan Open Meetings Act, Public Act 
267, Section 8 (c), the Troy Board of Education shall go into closed session to discuss 
negotiations.

BUSINESS 
2013 Summer Tax 
the Troy School District’s Board of Education intends 
to continue summer tax collections for the 2013-2014 
fiscal year and will notify the City of Troy of this 
action and that a copy of the attached resolution be 
incorporated as part of th e official minutes of the 
Board of Education. 
TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
TROY, MICHIGAN 48098 
ANNUAL SUMMER TAX RESOLUTION 
 WHEREAS, this Board of Education by 
resolution of November 13, 2012, determined to 
impose a summer property tax levy to collect one-
half of school property taxes, including debt services, 
upon property located within the Troy School District, 
beginning with 2013 and co ntinuing from year to 
year until specifically revoked by this Board of 
Education. 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
1. This Board of Education, pursuant to 1976
P.A. 451, as amended, hereby invokes a summer tax 
levy of one-half of school property taxes, including 
debt service, continuing with 2013, and continuing 
from year to year until sp ecifically revoked by this 
Board of Education, and requ ests each city in which 
this district is located an d in which a local school 
district or city is concurrently imposing a summer tax 
levy to collect those summer taxes.

collection of the District’s  summer tax levy that the

proposed agreement shall be brought before this 
Board for its approval or disapproval.

resolution be and the same are hereby rescinded.

WHEREAS, Section 22f of the State School Aid Act 
provides $52 per pupil one-time grants to districts 
that satisfy at least 7 of 8 best practices criteria not 
later than June 1, 2013 (MCL 388.1622f); and; 
WHEREAS, the Board of Education of Troy School 
District desires to receive  the $52 per pupil incentive 
payment; and; 
WHEREAS, the Troy School District has satisfied at 
least 7 of 8 best practices criteria; and; 
WHEREAS, eligibility for the incentive payment is 
contingent upon adopting a resolution that states the 
District has complied with at least 7 of 8 of the best 
practices criteria;  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED AS 
FOLLOWS: 
A. The Board of Education of Troy School District 
certifies that the District has complied with 
the following requirements: 
1) The District is the designated policy 
holder for medical benefit plan(s) 
pursuant to Section 22f(2)(a). 
2) The District has obtained a competitive 
bid on non-instructional services pursuant 
to Section 22f(2)(b). 
i. This includes custodial services,  
transportation and food services. 
3) The district accepts applications for 
enrollment by non-resident applicants 
under Section 105 or 105c (MCL 
388.1705) pursuant to Section 22f(2)(c). 
4) The district submits to the Michigan 
Department of Education a plan that 
shows progress toward developing the 
technology infrastructure necessary for 
the implementation of pupil academic 
Troy Board of Education Meeting 4 November 13, 2012 
10
Best Practices Incentive  
12-100 
It was moved by Mr. Miletti and supported by Ms. 
Underwood that  
growth assessments by 2014-2015 
pursuant to Section 22f(2)(d).  
5) The district supports opportunities for 
pupils to receive postsecondary credit 
while attending secondary school 
pursuant to Section 22f(2)(e). 
i. The district supports attendance of 
district pupils under the 
postsecondary enrollment options act, 
MCL 388.511 to 388.524, or under the 
career and technical preparation act, 
MCL 388.1901 to 388.1913, consistent 
with the provisions under section 21b. 
ii. The district offers college-level 
equivalent courses, as defined in 
section 1471 of the revised school 
code, MCL 380.1471. 
iii. The district provides other 
opportunities to pupils that allow 
those pupils to graduate with a high 
school diploma and also complete 
coursework that a postsecondary 
institution normally applies toward 
satisfaction of degree requirements. 
6) The district offers online instructional 
programs to all eligible pupils pursuant to 
Section 22f(2)(f). 
7) The District provides a link on the 
District’s home page to the URL for the 
MiSchool Data Portal which contains the 
required dashboard indicators pursuant 
to Section 22f(2)(g).  If certain data 
elements for our District are unavailable 
from State data collections, we agree to 
provide those data in the form and 
manner determined by the MDE.  The 
following indicators are included: 
i. Graduation and dropout rates. 
ii. Average class size in grades 
kindergarten to 3. 
Michigan merit examination test 
scores.

B. The Board of Education of Troy School District 
authorizes and directs its secretary to file this 
resolution with the State Aid and School 
Finance Office of the Mi chigan Department of

C. All resolutions and parts of resolutions insofar 
as they conflict with th e provisions of this 
resolution are hereby rescinded.

The purpose of the Troy School District (TSD) Troy 
Educator Appraisal Model (TEAM) is to maintain a 
climate that ensures quality instruction and enhances 
the professional growth of all members of our 
professional community. The goal of this process is to 
ensure continuous improvement through the use of: 
 Research-based instructional practice 
 Staff development aligned to common core and 
other nationally-recognized curricular expectations 
 Instructional leaders that promote and support 
instructional best practice
